> AGENT

Yes. The existing Twitter/X path is already in the repo:

- Admin can post immediately via `share_to_social` in [src/features/admin_chat/tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/tools.py).
- Posting itself runs through [src/features/sharing/sharer.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/sharing/sharer.py) and [src/features/sharing/subfeatures/social_poster.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/sharing/subfeatures/social_poster.py).
- Daily “social picks” drafts already exist, but they only DM suggestions to admin; they do not queue or publish. That lives in [src/features/summarising/summariser.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/summarising/summariser.py).

High-level plan:

1. Add a persistent social queue table.
   Fields: `id`, `guild_id`, `platform`, `post_type` (`tweet`, `reply`, `retweet`), `source_message_id`, `tweet_text`, `target_tweet_id`, `text_only`, `status`, `scheduled_for`, `posted_at`, `platform_post_id`, `platform_post_url`, `attempt_count`, `last_error`, `created_by`.

2. Add DB methods in [src/common/db_handler.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/common/db_handler.py).
   Needed operations: enqueue, list pending, claim due item, mark posted, mark failed, cancel, reschedule.

3. Add a scheduler cog.
   Follow the existing loop pattern from [src/features/competition/competition_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/competition/competition_cog.py) or [src/features/summarising/summariser_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/summarising/summariser_cog.py).
   Run every minute, pick due queue items, execute them, and update status.

4. Reuse the current post pipeline for normal tweets and replies.
   For `tweet` and `reply`, call the existing `Sharer.finalize_sharing(...)` path.
   That keeps media download, caption handling, dedupe, and shared-post recording in one place.

5. Add explicit retweet support.
   There is no retweet/repost implementation today.
   Add a `retweet_tweet(tweet_id)` function in [src/features/sharing/subfeatures/social_poster.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/sharing/subfeatures/social_poster.py), then let queued items of type `retweet` call that instead of `post_tweet`.

6. Add admin-chat queue tools instead of building UI first.
   Extend [src/features/admin_chat/tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/tools.py) with:
   - `schedule_social_post`
   - `list_scheduled_social_posts`
   - `cancel_scheduled_social_post`
   - `post_next_scheduled_social_post`
   This is the fastest path because admin chat already has the right permissions and context.

7. Model “post one per day” as queue semantics, not recurrence.
   Simplest version: you enqueue a batch in order, each with `scheduled_for`, or you store an ordered queue and a daily posting window, and the scheduler posts the next pending item each day.
   That fits your “queue up a bunch of assets and tweets and post them every day” requirement better than a generic cron system.

8. Hook social picks into the queue later.
   After the base queue works, let the admin promote a pick from the daily DM flow into the queue with one action, instead of copy-pasting.

9. Add basic safeguards.
   Idempotency per queue item, retry with backoff, success/failure logging, and a Discord/admin notification when a scheduled post fails.

If I were implementing this, I’d do it in this order: queue table + DB methods, scheduler cog, admin-chat scheduling tools, retweet support, then social-picks-to-queue integration.

**Light Plan**
# Implementation Plan: Scheduled Twitter/X Queue

## Overview
The repo already has a working “post now” Twitter/X path through [tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/tools.py), [sharer.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/sharing/sharer.py), and [social_poster.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/sharing/subfeatures/social_poster.py). What’s missing is persistent queue state, a timed worker, and explicit retweet support. The simplest shape is: keep the current post pipeline for tweets/replies, add a queue table plus scheduler, and expose queue controls through admin chat first instead of building UI.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Add queue persistence (`src/common/db_handler.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a new persisted queue model for scheduled social items with fields for platform, post type (`tweet`, `reply`, `retweet`), source Discord message, custom tweet text, target tweet ID, schedule time, status, attempts, and result metadata.
2. Add DB methods to enqueue, list pending, claim due items, mark posted, mark failed, cancel, and reschedule.
3. Keep queue items immutable after posting except for status/result fields so retries and audit are straightforward.

### Step 2: Add the scheduler worker (`src/features/.../social_scheduler_cog.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a new cog using the existing `@tasks.loop(minutes=1)` pattern already used elsewhere in the repo.
2. On each tick, fetch due queue items, claim one safely, execute it, and persist success/failure.
3. Add retry rules and a terminal failed state so bad items do not block the queue.

### Step 3: Reuse the existing posting path for tweets and replies (`src/features/sharing/sharer.py`, `src/features/admin_chat/tools.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Route queued `tweet` and `reply` items through `Sharer.finalize_sharing(...)` so media download, dedupe, shared-post tracking, and announcement behavior stay centralized.
2. Preserve the existing `text_only` behavior for thread replies.
3. Treat queue execution as a first-class caller rather than duplicating posting logic.

### Step 4: Add explicit retweet support (`src/features/sharing/subfeatures/social_poster.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Add a dedicated retweet/repost function for X/Twitter by target tweet ID.
2. Wire queued `retweet` items to that function instead of `post_tweet(...)`.
3. Store the returned platform metadata in the same shared-post or queue result fields used for normal posts.

### Step 5: Add admin controls for queueing (`src/features/admin_chat/tools.py`, `src/features/admin_chat/agent.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add admin-chat tools to schedule a post, list queued posts, cancel one, and optionally run the next due item manually.
2. Support batch enqueue for “post one per day” by accepting a sequence of assets/posts and assigning sequential daily `scheduled_for` timestamps.
3. Keep this as the first control surface; defer any GUI until the workflow is proven.

### Step 6: Connect social-picks later, not first (`src/features/summarising/summariser.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Leave the existing DM-based social picks flow in place initially.
2. Add a second-step path where a pick can be promoted into the queue, instead of making picks auto-publish on day one.
3. Keep generation and scheduling separate until the queue is stable.

### Step 7: Validate the behavior
**Scope:** Small
1. Add focused coverage for queue state transitions and due-item claiming.
2. Add targeted tests for queued tweet, queued reply, and queued retweet execution paths.
3. Manually smoke test: enqueue 2-3 items, verify daily cadence, verify retry/failure handling, and confirm duplicate prevention on reruns.

## Execution Order
1. Queue storage and DB methods.
2. Scheduler cog and due-item claiming.
3. Queued tweet/reply execution via existing sharer flow.
4. Retweet support.
5. Admin-chat scheduling tools.
6. Social-picks integration.

## Validation Order
1. DB and queue-state tests.
2. Scheduler happy-path and failure-path tests.
3. Manual end-to-end check against the existing Twitter/X credentials.

**Assumptions**
- “Every day” means a simple once-per-day cadence, not arbitrary cron rules.
- Admin chat is the right first control surface.
- Retweets should be scheduled by tweet ID/URL, not derived from Discord content.
- No public/community self-serve queue UI is needed in the first version.

**Success Criteria**
- `must`: An admin can enqueue a tweet or reply and have it post automatically when `scheduled_for` is reached.
- `must`: An admin can enqueue a retweet/repost by tweet ID/URL and have it execute on schedule.
- `must`: Failed scheduled items move to a visible failed state and do not block later queue items.
- `must`: Queue execution reuses the existing share pipeline for tweet/reply posting instead of duplicating media/post logic.
- `should`: Admin chat can batch-schedule a list of posts into a daily queue in one action.
- `should`: Queue items and outcomes are inspectable enough to debug without reading raw logs.
- `info`: A later follow-up may connect daily social picks directly into this queue.

If you approve, I’ll implement against this shape and keep the first pass admin-chat only.

# Implementation Plan: Unified Social Publishing

## Overview
The repo already has a working immediate Twitter/X path through [src/features/admin_chat/tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/tools.py), [src/features/sharing/sharer.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/sharing/sharer.py), and [src/features/sharing/subfeatures/social_poster.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/sharing/subfeatures/social_poster.py). The right implementation is not “add a scheduler beside it.” It is to introduce a single social dispatch layer with two modes: `immediate` and `scheduled`. Existing “post now” flows keep working by calling the new dispatcher in immediate mode. Scheduled posting becomes the same dispatch request, persisted and executed later. The design should also stop being Twitter-specific at the orchestration level, so later destinations can be added without redoing the queue and tracking model.

## Phase 1: Foundation

### Step 1: Define the social domain model
**Scope:** Medium  
1. Add a platform-agnostic queue table, for example `social_dispatch_queue`, with fields for `id`, `guild_id`, `source_channel_id`, `source_message_id`, `destination_key`, `platform`, `action_type`, `mode`, `payload`, `scheduled_for`, `status`, `attempt_count`, `last_error`, `created_by`, `created_at`, `claimed_at`, `completed_at`.
2. Add a separate delivery/attempt history table, for example `social_dispatch_attempts`, so success/failure is tracked per attempt rather than overwriting one row.
3. Add a destination/routing config table, for example `social_destinations`, so outbound targets are explicit and not hardcoded to “Twitter.”
4. Keep `shared_posts` for existing Twitter success/deletion compatibility in v1 rather than forcing an immediate migration.

### Step 2: Define routing and destination configuration
**Scope:** Medium  
1. Model destinations as named configs like `twitter_main`, `twitter_alt`, `discord_announcements`, not as raw platform strings.
2. Allow routing by source Discord channel so different source channels can default to different outbound destinations later.
3. Support manual override in admin tools so an admin can choose a destination directly even if routing rules exist.

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Introduce a unified dispatcher
**Scope:** Medium  
1. Add a new service, for example `social_dispatcher.py`, responsible for validating a request, resolving destination config, and either dispatching immediately or enqueueing for later.
2. Treat actions as typed operations: `post`, `reply`, `retweet`.
3. Keep platform-specific execution behind adapters so Twitter/X becomes one adapter, not the system itself.

### Step 4: Preserve current immediate mode
**Scope:** Medium  
1. Change the existing admin path in [src/features/admin_chat/tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/tools.py) so `share_to_social` routes through the new dispatcher with `mode="immediate"`.
2. Keep current behavior for all existing tweets: same defaults, same media handling, same reply behavior, same dedupe semantics where appropriate.
3. Reuse [src/features/sharing/sharer.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/sharing/sharer.py) for `post` and `reply` so the new architecture wraps current logic instead of replacing it.

### Step 5: Add scheduled mode
**Scope:** Medium  
1. Add scheduler execution as a cog with a `tasks.loop(minutes=1)` worker that claims due queue items and executes them.
2. Make claiming atomic enough that duplicate execution does not happen if the bot restarts or two loops overlap.
3. Add retries with bounded backoff and a terminal failed state.

### Step 6: Add Twitter/X retweet support
**Scope:** Small  
1. Add a retweet/repost function in [src/features/sharing/subfeatures/social_poster.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/sharing/subfeatures/social_poster.py).
2. Route `action_type="retweet"` through the Twitter adapter instead of the existing `post_tweet` flow.
3. Store returned tweet metadata in the delivery record and, if needed, the legacy `shared_posts` table for consistency.

## Phase 3: Admin Controls

### Step 7: Expand admin tools
**Scope:** Medium  
1. Add tools to schedule a post, list scheduled posts, cancel/reschedule a post, and manually run a queued item.
2. Accept both immediate and scheduled requests through one interface, with `scheduled_for` deciding whether the request posts now or queues.
3. Support batch queueing so an admin can line up multiple items to post daily.

### Step 8: Add destination-aware commands
**Scope:** Medium  
1. Let admin tools specify `destination_key`, not just “Twitter.”
2. Add commands to inspect configured destinations and routing rules.
3. Keep Twitter/X as the only fully implemented outbound destination in the first pass, but make the command schema destination-aware immediately.

## Phase 4: Tracking and Observability

### Step 9: Track success, failure, and audit state
**Scope:** Medium  
1. Record every attempt with result, error summary, timestamps, and returned platform IDs/URLs.
2. Expose queue status and recent delivery outcomes in admin chat so failures are visible without log-diving.
3. Send a Discord/admin notification when a scheduled delivery exhausts retries.

### Step 10: Keep delete/lookup compatibility
**Scope:** Small  
1. Preserve current immediate Twitter delete behavior by continuing to write successful Twitter posts into `shared_posts`.
2. Add a clear bridge from new delivery records to existing deletion code so current cleanup features do not regress.
3. Defer any full `shared_posts` migration until after the unified system is stable.

## Phase 5: Validation

### Step 11: Add targeted tests
**Scope:** Medium  
1. Cover dispatch request validation, queue insertion, due-item claiming, retry transitions, and destination resolution.
2. Cover immediate `post`, immediate `reply`, scheduled `post`, scheduled `reply`, and scheduled `retweet`.
3. Add restart/idempotency checks for claimed and completed items.

### Step 12: Run end-to-end smoke checks
**Scope:** Small  
1. Verify current immediate Twitter posting still works unchanged.
2. Verify a queued daily batch posts one item per day in order.
3. Verify failed items surface clearly and do not block later items.

## Execution Order
1. Create the new domain tables and DB methods first.
2. Add the dispatcher second.
3. Repoint existing immediate posting to the dispatcher before adding scheduling.
4. Add the scheduler and retry logic.
5. Add retweet support.
6. Add admin queue/destination controls.
7. Finish with tracking polish and tests.

## Validation Order
1. DB and queue-state tests.
2. Dispatcher unit tests.
3. Scheduler execution tests.
4. Manual smoke test against the current Twitter/X credentials.

## Questions
- In v1, do you want only one X account with multiple logical destinations, or actual multiple outbound X accounts?
- When you say “extendable by channel,” should routing be based on source Discord channel, outbound social account/channel, or both?
- Do you want quote-tweets in scope now, or only plain retweets/reposts?

## Assumptions
- Existing immediate Twitter posting must remain available and should be preserved by wrapping, not rewriting, the current path.
- Twitter/X is the only implemented outbound adapter in v1.
- The first UI surface is admin chat, not a new web/admin panel.
- Batch scheduling means ordered queueing with explicit `scheduled_for` timestamps, not a general cron builder.

## Success Criteria
- `must`: Existing immediate Twitter posting still works through the new dispatcher without changing the admin workflow.
- `must`: An admin can queue scheduled `post`, `reply`, and `retweet` actions and have them execute at `scheduled_for`.
- `must`: Each queued item has visible status and attempt history, including success and failure details.
- `must`: Failed queue items do not execute repeatedly forever and do not block later due items.
- `must`: Destination resolution is abstracted from platform execution so new outbound destinations can be added without redesigning queue storage.
- `should`: Source Discord channels can be mapped to default destinations with admin override.
- `should`: Successful Twitter deliveries continue to interoperate with existing delete/lookup behavior.
- `info`: A later phase can plug social-picks output directly into the same queue.

If you approve this shape, I’d implement it as “dispatcher first, scheduler second” rather than starting with a Twitter queue in isolation.

> AGENT

`megaplan` is blocked in its agent backend, not in your repo.

What I observed:

- The config routes planning through `codex` by default in [config.json](/Users/user_c042661f/.config/megaplan/config.json).
- The `codex`-routed plan attempt failed with backend/network errors recorded in [plan_v1_raw.txt](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/.megaplan/plans/add-scheduled-twitter-x-20260409-1556/plan_v1_raw.txt).
- That file shows:
  - DNS/WebSocket failures reaching `wss://chatgpt.com/backend-api/codex/responses`
  - repeated reconnect attempts
  - then a `429` rate-limit result
- I then forced `megaplan` to use `claude` instead of `codex`. That launched a live `claude -p ...` subprocess, but it never returned a structured plan and never wrote plan artifacts.
- The plan state stayed `initialized` in [state.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/.megaplan/plans/implement-a-unified-social-20260409-1710/state.json), which means the wrapper was waiting on the external planner and never got a valid result.

So the actual blockers are:

1. `codex` path: backend connectivity/rate-limit failure.
2. `claude` path: subprocess hang/no returned artifact.

> DEVELOPER

Why is condex not working though? What's actually going wrong?

> AGENT

`codex` is failing before it ever gets to your repo logic. The problem is in the `megaplan -> codex backend` hop.

What the raw failure shows in [plan_v1_raw.txt](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/.megaplan/plans/add-scheduled-twitter-x-20260409-1556/plan_v1_raw.txt):

- It tries to open a websocket to `wss://chatgpt.com/backend-api/codex/responses`
- DNS lookup fails: `failed to lookup address information: nodename nor servname provided, or not known`
- It retries several times
- Then the stream dies on the HTTPS fallback too: `stream disconnected before completion`
- `megaplan` records the run as a `429`/rate-limit style failure in [state.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/.megaplan/plans/add-scheduled-twitter-x-20260409-1556/state.json)

So the actual failure chain is:

1. `megaplan` is configured to use `codex` for planning in [config.json](/Users/user_c042661f/.config/megaplan/config.json).
2. `codex` starts normally.
3. `codex` cannot reliably resolve or connect to its backend at `chatgpt.com`.
4. Because the backend connection never stabilizes, the planner never returns a valid JSON artifact.
5. `megaplan` therefore stays stuck at `initialized` or marks the run as failed.

There are also Rust panic lines in the raw output about `system-configuration` and telemetry exporter setup. Those look like secondary startup noise after the network/backend failure, not the primary cause.

So, concretely: `codex` is not broken on your codebase. It is failing on outbound backend connectivity, and in one attempt it also hit rate limiting.

> AGENT

I looked through `Documents/megaplan`, and the main finding is:

`codex` itself is not generally broken. `megaplan` is misdiagnosing one class of Codex failures, and the actual failure in your saved run was a backend transport/DNS problem.

What’s actually happening:

- `megaplan` launches Codex very simply in [workers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/workers.py#L880): `codex exec --skip-git-repo-check -C <project> -o <tmpfile> --output-schema <schema> -`
- I reproduced that subprocess pattern directly outside `megaplan`.
- A trivial Codex call succeeds.
- So the Codex binary, PATH, auth context, and basic subprocess invocation are all fine.

The real saved failure is in [plan_v1_raw.txt](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/.megaplan/plans/add-scheduled-twitter-x-20260409-1556/plan_v1_raw.txt#L119):
- repeated DNS lookup failures for `wss://chatgpt.com/backend-api/codex/responses`
- then `stream disconnected before completion`
- so that run died while trying to talk to the Codex backend

The misleading part is a real `megaplan` bug in [workers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/workers.py#L131):
- `_CODEX_ERROR_PATTERNS` includes a bare `"429"` substring match
- `_diagnose_codex_failure()` returns the first substring match it sees
- your raw failure file contains thread IDs like `42967821` near the top
- so `megaplan` incorrectly reported “Codex hit a rate limit (HTTP 429)” even though the actual error was connection/DNS failure

So the short answer is:

1. Codex works for simple requests.
2. Your failed megaplan run did not fail because of the repo or the launcher.
3. It failed because Codex couldn’t reach its backend reliably in that run.
4. `megaplan` then mislabeled that failure as `429` due to a bad substring matcher.

There is one more practical observation:
- when I reproduced the real megaplan planning prompt, Codex didn’t fail instantly, but it also didn’t finish quickly; it stalled long enough to time out in my reproduction.
- That suggests the heavy planning request is more sensitive to backend instability than a trivial Codex call.

If you want, I can patch `Documents/megaplan` so it reports Codex failures correctly:
- remove the bare `"429"` matcher
- add explicit matches for `failed to lookup address information`
- add explicit matches for `stream disconnected before completion`
- surface those as `connection_error` instead of fake rate-limit errors

> AGENT

Patched in `Documents/megaplan`:

- Fixed Codex failure classification in [workers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/workers.py#L131).
- Added regression tests in [test_workers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/tests/test_workers.py#L894).

What changed:
- Transport failures like `failed to lookup address information`, `failed to connect to websocket`, and `stream disconnected before completion` now map to `connection_error`.
- The bad bare `"429"` substring match is gone, so thread IDs like `42967821` no longer get mislabeled as rate limits.
- Real HTTP 429s are still recognized.

Verification:
- `uv run pytest tests/test_workers.py -q`
- Result: `53 passed`

Rerun result:
- I reran `megaplan plan` with the patched local CLI and `--agent codex`.
- It no longer failed immediately with the fake 429 diagnosis.
- But it still did not complete: after a long wait the Codex subprocess was still running, the temp output file was still empty, and no plan artifact was written.
- I stopped the hanging process.

So:
- The diagnosis bug is fixed.
- `codex` still does not reliably complete this real planning request in `megaplan`.
- The remaining issue is not the fake 429 classification anymore; it’s that the Codex planning call can stall for this workload and never produce output.

If you want, I can make one more practical patch to `megaplan` next:
1. capture partial Codex stdout/stderr to the raw artifact while the process is running, and/or
2. add a shorter planning timeout plus a clearer `worker_timeout`/`connection_error` handoff so stalled Codex runs fail fast instead of hanging.

> AGENT

I found the real root cause.

The Codex worker is recursively invoking `megaplan` from inside the `megaplan plan` step.

Evidence from the clean-env timeout capture:
- while handling the plan prompt, Codex opened the `megaplan` skill
- then it ran:
  - `megaplan config show`
  - `megaplan init ...`
  - `megaplan plan ...`
- so the planner is nesting a second `megaplan` run inside the first one

That recursion explains the hangs and likely the later backend instability. I’m patching `Documents/megaplan` now to make Codex planning prompts explicitly forbid recursive `megaplan` CLI use and skill re-entry.

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/workers.py\n@@\n def run_command(\n     command: list[str],\n     *,\n     cwd: Path,\n     stdin_text: str | None = None,\n+    env: dict[str, str] | None = None,\n     timeout: int | None = None,\n ) -> CommandResult:\n@@\n         process = subprocess.run(\n             command,\n             cwd=str(cwd),\n             input=stdin_text,\n             text=True,\n             capture_output=True,\n+            env=env,\n             timeout=timeout,\n         )\n@@\n def _codex_timeout_for_step(step: str) -> int:\n@@\n     return min(configured_timeout, _CODEX_NON_EXECUTE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS)\n+\n+\n+def _codex_child_env() -> dict[str, str]:\n+    env = os.environ.copy()\n+    # Nested Codex workers should not inherit the parent Codex session state.\n+    # Those variables can cause the child to attach to the outer thread/CI\n+    # context instead of behaving like an isolated worker invocation.\n+    env.pop(\"CODEX_THREAD_ID\", None)\n+    env.pop(\"CODEX_CI\", None)\n+    return env\n@@\n-        result = run_command(command, cwd=Path.cwd(), stdin_text=prompt, timeout=timeout_seconds)\n+        result = run_command(\n+            command,\n+            cwd=Path.cwd(),\n+            stdin_text=prompt,\n+            env=_codex_child_env(),\n+            timeout=timeout_seconds,\n+        )\n*** End Patch\n"
}

> AGENT

Got to the bottom of it. `megaplan` was failing for four separate reasons, not one.

The actual causes were:
- `megaplan` misdiagnosed Codex failures as 429s because [workers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/workers.py) matched any raw `"429"` substring, including thread IDs.
- nested Codex workers were inheriting parent Codex session env (`CODEX_THREAD_ID`, `CODEX_CI`), which caused session contamination and inconsistent backend behavior.
- the Codex planning prompt was too loose, so the worker could wander into unrelated repo docs and spend its budget answering the wrong problem instead of returning schema JSON.
- after fixing those, the remaining blocker was self-inflicted: the non-execute Codex timeout in [workers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/workers.py) was only 180s, but the real planning call needed more than that.

What I changed:
- fixed Codex error classification and timeout handling in [workers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/workers.py)
- sanitized child Codex env in [workers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/workers.py)
- added the nested-harness guard in [prompts/__init__.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/prompts/__init__.py)
- tightened plan-step focus rules in [prompts/planning.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/prompts/planning.py)
- added regressions in [test_workers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/tests/test_workers.py) and [test_prompts.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/tests/test_prompts.py)

Verification:
- `uv run pytest tests/test_prompts.py tests/test_workers.py -q`
- result: `103 passed`
- direct raw `codex exec --ephemeral --output-schema ...` reproduction now produces valid JSON
- real `megaplan plan --plan implement-a-unified-social-20260409-1710 --agent codex --ephemeral` now succeeds

Current state:
- plan status is `planned` in [state.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/.megaplan/plans/implement-a-unified-social-20260409-1710/state.json)
- artifacts were written: [plan_v1.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/.megaplan/plans/implement-a-unified-social-20260409-1710/plan_v1.md) and [plan_v1.meta.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/.megaplan/plans/implement-a-unified-social-20260409-1710/plan_v1.meta.json)
- next step is `critique`

One note: the `megaplan` repo worktree also shows a modified `uv.lock` plus two unrelated untracked critique outputs. I did not revert those.

`review.rework_items[].flag_id` and `source` intentionally stay optional.\n+    # `review.rework_items[]` uses explicit required fields because OpenAI\n+    # structured outputs require every property key to appear in `required`.\n     return path[-3:] == (\"properties\", \"rework_items\", \"items\")\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/.megaplan/schemas/review.json\n@@\n           \"evidence_file\": {\n             \"type\": \"string\"\n           },\n           \"flag_id\": {\n-            \"type\": \"string\"\n+            \"type\": [\n+              \"string\",\n+              \"null\"\n+            ]\n           },\n           \"source\": {\n-            \"type\": \"string\"\n+            \"type\": [\n+              \"string\",\n+              \"null\"\n+            ]\n           }\n         },\n         \"required\": [\n           \"task_id\",\n           \"issue\",\n           \"expected\",\n           \"actual\",\n-          \"evidence_file\"\n+          \"evidence_file\",\n+          \"flag_id\",\n+          \"source\"\n         ],\n         \"additionalProperties\": false\n       }\n     },\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/prompts/review.py\n@@\n         - `rework_items` must be an array of structured rework directives. When `review_verdict` is `needs_rework`, populate one entry per issue with:\n           - `task_id`: which finalize task this issue relates to\n           - `issue`: what is wrong\n           - `expected`: what correct behavior looks like\n           - `actual`: what was observed\n           - `evidence_file` (optional): file path supporting the finding\n+          - `flag_id`: critique/review flag ID when applicable, otherwise `null`\n+          - `source`: short machine-readable source tag when applicable, otherwise `null`\n         - `issues` must still be populated as a flat one-line-per-item summary derived from `rework_items` (for backward compatibility). > AGENT

You queue them through `share_to_social` with `schedule_for`.

Practical shape:
- Normal queued post:
  - `message_link` or `message_id`
  - optional `tweet_text`
  - `schedule_for` as ISO-8601 with timezone, e.g. `2026-04-10T14:00:00Z`
- Queued reply:
  - same, plus `action: "reply"` and `target_post` set to the X post URL or ID
- Queued retweet:
  - `action: "retweet"`
  - `target_post` set to the X post URL or ID
  - no `tweet_text`

Examples:
```json
{
  "message_link": "https://discord.com/channels/123/456/789",
  "tweet_text": "New drop from the server.",
  "schedule_for": "2026-04-10T14:00:00Z"
}
```

```json
{
  "message_link": "https://discord.com/channels/123/456/789",
  "action": "reply",
  "target_post": "https://x.com/someone/status/1911111111111111111",
  "tweet_text": "Following up on this.",
  "schedule_for": "2026-04-10T15:00:00Z"
}
```

```json
{
  "message_link": "https://discord.com/channels/123/456/789",
  "action": "retweet",
  "target_post": "https://x.com/someone/status/1911111111111111111",
  "schedule_for": "2026-04-10T16:00:00Z"
}
```

What you get back:
- immediate post: `tweet_url`, `tweet_id`, `publication_id`
- scheduled post: `publication_id` and `status: "queued"`

How to inspect the queue:
- query `social_publications` with `query_table`
- useful filters: `status`, `action`, `platform`, `message_id`
- statuses are `queued`, `processing`, `succeeded`, `failed`, `cancelled`

Example queue inspection:
```json
{
  "table": "social_publications",
  "filters": {
    "status": "queued"
  },
  "order": "scheduled_at",
  "limit": 25
}
```

Important caveat:
- this is queueing one item at a time right now
- if you want “load 30 posts and drip one per day,” we should add a small batch helper on top of this

Relevant code is in [tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/tools.py), [social_publish_service.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/sharing/social_publish_service.py), and the queue schema in [social_publications.sql](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/sql/social_publications.sql).

If you want, I can sketch the exact admin-chat prompts you’d use day to day.

> AGENT

Mostly yes at the app layer, but not fully hardened at the DB layer.

What is protected:
- `share_to_social` is only exposed to admin chat, not member chat. Non-admin users get `MEMBER_TOOLS`, which excludes it in [tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/tools.py).
- Admin status is a strict `ADMIN_USER_ID` check in [admin_chat_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/admin_chat_cog.py).
- So through the bot UI, only the configured admin can queue new rows.

What is not hardened:
- The checked-in SQL for [social_publications.sql](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/sql/social_publications.sql) creates tables/functions, but I do not see RLS or explicit DB policies/grants for these tables.
- The bot uses the Supabase service key in admin tooling, so if someone has direct service-role DB access, they could insert rows outside admin chat.
- The scheduler will trust queued rows in `social_publications`; it does not verify “this row was created by an admin tool” before executing.

So the accurate answer is:
- Bot-facing path: admin-only
- Database-facing path: not locked down by this change alone

If you want this to be properly secure end-to-end, the next hardening pass should add:
1. RLS/policies or tightly scoped RPC-only writes for `social_publications` and `social_channel_routes`
2. a creator/source audit field and validation on execution
3. a rule that only bot-written rows in allowed states can be executed

If you want, I can make that hardening pass next.

> AGENT

I did it directly; this didn’t need a new megaplan.

The hardening is in:
- Bot path is still admin-only through [admin_chat_cog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/admin_chat_cog.py) and [tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/tools.py).
- Scheduled publication rows are now signed in [social_publish_service.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/sharing/social_publish_service.py).
- The scheduler refuses to execute tampered or unsigned rows, and delete also refuses invalid rows.
- The SQL artifact in [social_publications.sql](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/sql/social_publications.sql) now adds `integrity_version` / `integrity_signature`, enables RLS on the two social tables, and revokes access from `anon` / `authenticated`.

What that means in practice:
- Only the configured admin can enqueue through the bot UI.
- Even if someone inserts a fake queued row directly in the DB, the bot will not execute it unless the row has a valid bot-generated signature.
- This closes the main “DB row injection -> scheduler posts to X” path.

Verification:
- `python -m pytest tests/ -q`
- Result: `19 passed`

One operational note: you need to apply [social_publications.sql](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/sql/social_publications.sql) in Supabase for the DB-side hardening to take effect. The signing logic uses `SOCIAL_PUBLISH_SIGNING_SECRET` if set, otherwise falls back to `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`.

If you want, I can do one more pass to add explicit admin-only route-management tools instead of relying on direct DB edits for `social_channel_routes`.

> DEVELOPER

Will that strengthen it?

> AGENT

Yes.

Right now the weak point is route management: `social_channel_routes` exists, but changing it is basically a DB operation. Adding explicit admin-only tools for route create/update/delete would strengthen things because:

- route changes would go through the same admin gate as queue creation
- you’d get a controlled input shape instead of arbitrary DB edits
- it reduces the chance of accidental bad routes or someone using generic DB access in the wrong way
- it gives you an auditable, intentional surface for “which channel posts to which outbound route”

What it would not replace:
- the row-signing hardening I just added
- DB/service-role security in Supabase

So the security order is:

1. Admin-only bot tools for route changes
2. Signed publication rows so injected queue items do not execute
3. DB-side revokes/RLS so public client roles cannot touch these tables

That route-management tooling is worth doing, but it’s secondary to the signing fix. The big exploit path was fake queued rows getting executed; that’s the part we closed.

then it becomes light megaplan territory. For the current ask, no.

To clean up a channel: browse it first with find_messages(live=true), then pass the IDs to delete.\n - upload_file(channel_id, file_path, content?)\n - share_to_social(message_id or message_link, tweet_text?, action?, target_post?, reply_to_tweet?, text_only?, schedule_for?, platform?) — share or schedule publishing to X/Twitter. Supports post, reply, and retweet actions. `schedule_for` must be ISO-8601 with timezone. `target_post` or `reply_to_tweet` accepts a tweet URL or ID. **Thread replies default to text-only** so a follow-up doesn't reattach the parent tweet's media — pass `text_only=false` explicitly if you DO want a reply to also include the source message's media. Immediate success returns `tweet_url`/`tweet_id` plus `publication_id`; scheduled success returns `publication_id` with queued status. If `already_shared` is true on a rerun, cite the returned existing `tweet_url` instead of treating it as a failure.\n+- list_social_routes(platform?, channel_id?, enabled?, limit?) — inspect configured social routes for the active guild.\n+- create_social_route(platform?, channel_id?, enabled?, route_config?) — create a guild-default or channel-specific route. Omit channel_id for the guild default. Put outbound details in `route_config`, for example `{\"account\": \"main\"}`.\n+- update_social_route(route_id, platform?, channel_id?, enabled?, route_config?) — update one route by id. Pass `channel_id=\"\"` to convert it to the guild-default route.\n+- delete_social_route(route_id) — delete one route by id.\n - update_member_socials(user_id, twitter_url?, reddit_url?) — set/clear a member's Twitter or Reddit handle on file. The Twitter handle is what social picks use to auto-tag the creator. Pass an empty string to clear a field; omit a field to leave it alone. Use this whenever the admin says things like \"set X's twitter to @y\" or \"X is @y on twitter\".\n - resolve_user(username) — get a user's Discord ID and mention tag.\n@@\n-**Media.** When the user asks for a video, image, or any media item, ALWAYS include the actual attachment URL (the video/image file itself), not just a link to the Discord message that contains it. A message link doesn't answer \"show me the video\". Always call refresh_media=true (or use inspect_message) to get fresh CDN URLs, then put each media URL bare on its own line in its own message so Discord auto-embeds it. Optionally include the message link too if context (the post's caption, who shared it, reactions) is also relevant — but the media URL itself is the answer and must be there. send_message auto-refreshes CDN URLs. For scheduled or failed social publishing work, inspect `social_publications` and `social_channel_routes` with `query_table`.\n+**Media.** When the user asks for a video, image, or any media item, ALWAYS include the actual attachment URL (the video/image file itself), not just a link to the Discord message that contains it. A message link doesn't answer \"show me the video\". Always call refresh_media=true (or use inspect_message) to get fresh CDN URLs, then put each media URL bare on its own line in its own message so Discord auto-embeds it. Optionally include the message link too if context (the post's caption, who shared it, reactions) is also relevant — but the media URL itself is the answer and must be there. send_message auto-refreshes CDN URLs. For scheduled or failed social publishing work, inspect `social_publications` and use the dedicated social route tools before falling back to `query_table` on `social_channel_routes`.\n@@\n - Share to social media with `share_to_social`, including scheduled posts/replies/retweets. Use the returned `publication_id` for canonical tracking, and if `already_shared` is true, report the existing `tweet_url`.\n+- Manage channel-to-social routing with `list_social_routes`, `create_social_route`, `update_social_route`, and `delete_social_route` instead of editing `social_channel_routes` manually when possible.\n*** End Patch\n"

> AGENT

Done. This didn’t need a megaplan.

I added admin-only route-management tools in [tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/tools.py) and documented them in [agent.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/src/features/admin_chat/agent.py). You now have `list_social_routes`, `create_social_route`, `update_social_route`, and `delete_social_route`, all scoped to the active guild. `update_social_route` also lets you clear `channel_id` back to the guild default by passing an empty string.

This strengthens the system because route changes now go through the same admin-only chat surface as queue creation, instead of relying on raw `query_table` or direct DB edits. I also added focused coverage in [test_social_route_tools.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc/tests/test_social_route_tools.py). Verification passed with `python -m pytest tests/ -q`: `22 passed`.

Example shapes:
```json
{"platform":"twitter"}
```

```json
{"platform":"twitter","route_config":{"account":"main"}}
```

```json
{"route_id":"<route-id>","channel_id":"1234567890","route_config":{"account":"alt"}}
```

```json
{"route_id":"<route-id>"}
```
